# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-13

An expanded open-ended search across creative AI, with full guides completed from earlier screened entries and additional eligible discoveries retained in the searchable pending-profile appendix. This is documentation/source review; no discovered application was installed or run. First profiles do not mean a launch today. Quality labels reflect evidence, not popularity. Incomplete evidence remains Quality unverified; current prototype or unfinished-state evidence is required for Experimental. Software, model weights, assets, hosted services and required proprietary hosts are distinguished. All platforms are researched; each guide reports the supported route rather than inferring universal compatibility. Verified collection: 887/887 repository queries attempted; 887 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 85 bounded/truncated. All 25 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Empty model-source responses: text2text-generation. An empty response does not establish that these creative workflows have no tools. Separate from successful search queries, 45 watchlist fetches and 3 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation. Collection was resumed after an execution interruption before an observation was saved. Proven retained query/model responses and hash-matched README captures were reused with their capture times; the one missing repository query was attempted normally. Remaining repository, release and commit checks resumed live. No sealed edition or email was rebuilt or resent.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Field | Finding |
| --- | --- |
| Images & design | Invoke documents a canvas workflow for image generation and editing. Photon AI Studio is separately screened here as an open companion for a proprietary image-editor host; neither an attractive interface nor a local-processing claim establishes output quality. [Source 1](https://invoke.ai/) |
| Video, animation & film | OpenReelio presents its editing workflow on its own site. This edition completes several different local/cloud editing guides and records their actual service and hardware differences rather than treating every browser editor as offline. [Source 1](https://openreelio.com/) [Source 2](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source 3](https://phosphene.video/changelog/) |
| Audio, music & voice | OpenSuno describes YuE-based music work. ZONOS3-Mini has a current model card, but its full LICENSE request returned 404; a model-card Apache label alone was not accepted as a complete grant review. [Source 1](https://opensuno.org/) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/raw/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://www.zyphra.com/our-work/zonos3-mini) |
| 3D, reconstruction & assets | SolidCraft has its own current project site. The old SolveCraft repository redirects to the already-cataloged canonical project, so it was not added as a second new tool. [Source 1](https://solidcraft.io/) |
| Browser tools & web media | The WebLLM paper describes in-browser model inference. Browser inference is a possible implementation route, not evidence that every browser media tool supports every operating system or GPU. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15803) |
| WebXR, VR & AR | Meta documents agent-assisted Immersive Web SDK development, and Google publishes a current Three-Live announcement. WebXR authoring and actual headset compatibility remain separate checks. [Source 1](https://developers.meta.com/vr/blog/accelerate-vr-development-with-ai-and-immersive-web-sdk/) [Source 2](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html) |
| Computational art & creative coding | Fragment remains a useful creative-coding context, but conventional shader/live-code tools do not automatically qualify as AI software. Each admitted tool needs its own concrete model or agent workflow. [Source 1](https://fragment.tools/) |
| Interactive, immersive & live media | ossia describes interactive-installation workflows; SAT separately documents live generative-video tools. An interactive media feature alone is insufficient for AI eligibility. [Source 1](https://ossia.io/interactive-installations.html) [Source 2](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/) [Source 3](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/installation.html) |
| 3D printing & generative CAD | SOMA documents a Windows CAD preview, while Kerfline describes image-to-laser-SVG iteration. Mesh or vector export does not establish fabrication readiness or a successful physical cut. [Source 1](https://somacad.app/) [Source 2](https://www.kerfline.build/) |
| Games & production pipelines | ForgeAX documents agent-assisted game creation. Creative AI use, reproducible export and required game-host/service terms need separate evidence from general agent branding. [Source 1](https://forgeax.github.io/docs/) |
| Motion capture & character animation | Movens Zero presents motion-generation research. This edition also completes the Video Motion Customization guide, keeping the shipped combined train/infer route separate from promised standalone modes. [Source 1](https://movens-zero.github.io/) |
| Avatars, digital humans & lip sync | The AVTR-1 primary model page separates avatar-model artifacts from application code. Digital Human is profiled here with mock/default, cloud voice and optional real lip-sync routes clearly distinguished. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/avaturn-live/avtr-1/blob/main/README.md) |
| VFX, compositing & relighting | A primary Light-Aware Compositing PDF was captured, but a captured PDF is not a reproduced compositor or a full implementation/license review. No recommendation follows from the paper link alone. [Source 1](https://yaksoy.github.io/papers/SIG25p-LightAwareCompositing.pdf) |
| Spatial audio & volumetric media | The Neural Acoustic Fields PDF capture was bounded. A separate NSF primary publication record establishes the immersive-audio research context, while software availability and spatial audio reproduction remain unverified. [Source 1](https://seexpeng.github.io/data/papers/Neural%20Acoustic%20Fields%20for%20Mixed%20Reality.pdf) [Source 2](https://par.nsf.gov/biblio/10647880) |

Representation export is not proof of complete human capture, hardware compatibility or independently checked fidelity. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15719) |
| Editing, captions & post-production | Subtitle Edit maintains its own documentation. Caption Studio receives a full guide here with the actual MLX versus Linux/Docker transcription routes, port discrepancy and optional translation services disclosed. [Source 1](https://subtitleedit.github.io/subtitleedit/overview.html) |
| Vector graphics, illustration & textures | The SVGRender primary model organization was inspected as a discovery source. A model page does not by itself establish an installable, fully licensed vector-design application. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/SVGRender) |
| Typography, fonts & layout | VecGlypher has a primary research PDF, whose bounded capture was not treated as a full paper review. StoryGen receives a full guide; the recovered narrative query additionally led to source/grant-screened graph gamebooks, branching video stories and a visual fiction editor. Mock outputs and real provider calls remain distinct. | Photography, restoration & color | The primary restoration abstract addresses damaged historical color photographs. Restoration plausibility and faithful archival recovery are different claims; unseen details must not be presented as recovered facts. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22291) |

| Neural optical holography and computational displays | Stanford/Princeton primary pages document several neural holography approaches. SGWN Encoder is separately profiled for numerical phase encoding, with optical assumptions and checkpoint redistribution uncertainty explicit. [Source 1](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/neuralholography/) [Source 2](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/time-multiplexed-neural-holography/) [Source 3](https://light.princeton.edu/publication/artifact-resilient-holography/) [Source 4](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/odak/learn_models/) [Source 5](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/installation/) [Source 6](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/cgh/) [Source 7](https://cinescope-wkr.github.io/pado-hologram/) |
| AI poetry, kinetic literature and language installations | RiTa v4 explicitly describes itself as AI-free and was not admitted merely because it generates language. J-space theater and Neural Sonata instead expose concrete AI probability/hidden-state creative workflows; an empty jhave.com capture remains a gap. [Source 1](https://rednoise.org/rita/) [Source 2](https://jhave.com/) [Source 3](https://github.com/Cybefutu/j-space-theater) |
| Neural hair, fur and strand-groom authoring | HairDeck and its independent ComfyUI integration support a concrete image-to-hair-card workflow. HairGS combined non-commercial terms, missing grants and promised releases kept several other hair projects outside the eligible selection. [Source 1](https://github.com/yimin-pan/hair-gs) [Source 2](https://xiaokunsun.github.io/StrandHead.github.io/) [Source 3](https://haiminluo.github.io/hairgpt/) [Source 4](https://github.com/facebookresearch/iphg) |
| Neural smoke, fluid capture and editable VFX fields | HyFluid and PICT smoke reconstruction have actual research/code context, but complete software grants were not returned for the inspected repositories. Smoke demos or paper availability did not override this licensing gap. [Source 1](https://kovenyu.com/HyFluid/) [Source 2](https://github.com/19reborn/PICT_smoke) |

## Search and review counts

102 creative fields; 887/887 repository queries attempted; 25/25 model-task queries attempted. 56545 distinct source candidates and 1075 model leads. 41 detailed profiles and 159 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 85 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Current raw project documentation and grants were read for JoyAI MLX, openedit and an Xotion license. The first two are already profiled, so unchanged projects were not relisted. Xotion retains a complete PolyForm Noncommercial grant and is excluded. A live video-editor topic listing was a discovery aid, not license proof. [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/xotion-studio/-/raw/main/LICENSE)
- **codeberg** · searched: Imaginer metadata and the Raspberry Pi diffusion project were accessible. Broader diffusion/audio exploration requests returned HTTP 429; those failed attempts are archived and were not bypassed. Project availability is not a completed current license/setup review. [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/Imaginer/Imaginer)
- **sourcehut** · searched: The live diffusion project search returned diffront, diffuser and prs-search. The complete Diffuser MIT grant and Cargo manifest were read; its dependency points to an author-local absolute path, so portability remains a setup gap. The existing screened entry was not relisted unchanged. [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=diffusion) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/Cargo.toml) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **packages** · searched: Live npm/PyPI/NuGet metadata was archived, including litert-ocr 0.2.2, manimvox, brandly and TensorStack.Media through 0.14.8. Registry classifiers and license labels are discovery signals, not complete grants or independent quality evidence. No package was installed. [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/litert-ocr/latest) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/manimvox/0.2.0/json) [Source](https://api.nuget.org/v3-flatcontainer/tensorstack.media/index.json)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Krita and SAT primary documentation supplement plugin discovery. HairDeck integrations are screened for their actual node execution/export paths; underlying model terms and ComfyUI/platform requirements remain separate. A proprietary-host companion is labeled explicitly. [Source](https://docs.interstice.cloud/) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/koaia/installation.html)
- **project-sites** · searched: Current primary sites were fetched across all 102 observed fields. Several sites returned 404/TLS failures or empty shells and several PDFs were bounded; these are disclosed per field, not treated as complete implementation reviews. The pass is broad and bounded, not an exhaustive census. [Source](https://stitchfoundry.app/) [Source](https://midotronn.github.io/MAESTRO/) [Source](https://www.kerfline.build/)
- **research-code** · searched: Primary papers/project pages supplied hair, fluid, holography, tactile, motion and adjacent creative leads. Actual code and full grants were inspected separately: HairGS combined non-commercial terms and missing PICT/HyFluid grants blocked eligibility; code-coming-soon projects stayed context/watch findings. [Source](https://github.com/yimin-pan/hair-gs) [Source](https://kovenyu.com/HyFluid/) [Source](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/neuralholography/)
- **international** · searched: Korean/English print typography, Japanese VR HTML authoring, Chinese creator workflows and Arabic game-localization documentation were inspected. Native-language docs are not a reason for a weaker or stronger quality label; licenses, host services and platform recipes remain explicit. [Source](https://github.com/jojinkoo/print-linebreak-typography) [Source](https://github.com/wakufactory/AI_VR_Scene) [Source](https://abdallah28r.github.io/taarib/en/)

## Local AI Video Editor

Video, animation & film · Editing, captions & post-production · Audio, music & voice

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Turn permitted talking-head clips into a reviewable short with timed captions, reframing, graphics and a separate publish kit.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-13. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official README links a short editing GIF and screenshots. They document an example; Scout did not watch a live session or independently assess the output. [Source](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install Bun 1.3+, Node.js 20+ and a separately authenticated Claude Code. Clone, install dependencies, run setup and start the loopback-only editor. Setup downloads Windows media/model tools into the local application-data directory. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT application; Remotion has separate individual/small-team and company terms. FFmpeg GPL builds, whisper.cpp, YuNet, Kokoro and fonts retain their own terms. Claude/provider and optional image/stock services are separate. Local CPU/storage plus the creator’s own Claude plan/API usage. Optional image or stock-service accounts have independent terms. [Source](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The model service receives the information used by the agent; local media processing is not a promise of offline planning. Generated beats and stock-search results do not guarantee rights clearance or immunity to platform claims. Automatic edit quality and overnight reliability remain unverified.

### How it uses AI

A Windows editing room combines Claude-directed timeline changes with whisper.cpp transcription, face tracking, Kokoro narration and local FFmpeg/Chrome rendering. [Source](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A Windows editing room combines Claude-directed timeline changes with whisper.cpp transcription, face tracking, Kokoro narration and local FFmpeg/Chrome rendering. [Source 1](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Turn permitted talking-head clips into a reviewable short with timed captions, reframing, graphics and a separate publish kit. [Source 1](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The official README links a short editing GIF and screenshots. They document an example; Scout did not watch a live session or independently assess the output. [Source 1](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install Bun 1.3+, Node.js 20+ and a separately authenticated Claude Code. Clone, install dependencies, run setup and start the loopback-only editor. Setup downloads Windows media/model tools into the local application-data directory. [Source 1](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install Bun 1.3+, Node.js 20+ and a separately authenticated Claude Code. Clone, install dependencies, run setup and start the loopback-only editor. Setup downloads Windows media/model tools into the local application-data directory.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor.git
```


```sh
cd local-ai-video-editor
```


```sh
bun install
```


```sh
bun run setup
```


```sh
bun start
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open 127.0.0.1:4600, create a project and choose the desired aspect ratio.
2. Drop in your own clip, inspect the transcript and ask the Director for a short, specific edit.
3. Review captions, timing, preview frames and music permissions before exporting; keep automatic overnight jobs optional.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Documented minimum 8 GB RAM, recommended 16 GB; about 3 GB free disk for dependencies/tools/models plus working media. A universal VRAM minimum is not documented.
- **Software:** Bun 1.3+, Node.js 20+, Claude Code/account, downloaded FFmpeg and whisper.cpp. Headless Chrome handles rendering; Kokoro downloads on first narration use.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10/11 64-bit is the documented setup target; the setup downloads Windows binaries. Other native platforms are not established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT application; Remotion has separate individual/small-team and company terms. FFmpeg GPL builds, whisper.cpp, YuNet, Kokoro and fonts retain their own terms. Claude/provider and optional image/stock services are separate.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT application; Remotion has separate individual/small-team and company terms. FFmpeg GPL builds, whisper.cpp, YuNet, Kokoro and fonts retain their own terms. Claude/provider and optional image/stock services are separate.
- **Cost:** Local CPU/storage plus the creator’s own Claude plan/API usage. Optional image or stock-service accounts have independent terms.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The model service receives the information used by the agent; local media processing is not a promise of offline planning. Generated beats and stock-search results do not guarantee rights clearance or immunity to platform claims. Automatic edit quality and overnight reliability remain unverified. [Source 1](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

- [Repository](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/haidermoulay3mk/local-ai-video-editor/blob/main/README.md)

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Iterate on talking-head cuts, product films, reframing and code-authored motion graphics while retaining project history and before/after review.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-13. No local execution implied.

Native helpers, models, fonts, FFmpeg and any media assets have their own notices. Model keys or AgentShot credits do not come with the software grant. Local rendering resources plus separately chosen Claude, Gemini, OpenAI-compatible or credit-service usage. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Local analysis does not mean the language model stays offline: configured providers receive text and requested still frames. Phone access and the developer’s GPU/sandbox guarantees require their own platform checks. Numeric system RAM, VRAM and disk minima are not published in the reviewed README.

### How it uses AI

A conversational native editor turns locally measured words, shots, faces and beats into a language-model edit plan, then proposes an editable cut for human acceptance. [Source](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Introduction

A conversational native editor turns locally measured words, shots, faces and beats into a language-model edit plan, then proposes an editable cut for human acceptance. [Source 1](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### What it is good for

Iterate on talking-head cuts, product films, reframing and code-authored motion graphics while retaining project history and before/after review. [Source 1](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Demo & examples

The README publishes a short cat-footage demonstration and interface screenshots. That developer demonstration does not establish independent reproduction or permission to reuse its footage. [Source 1](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Install

Choose the official desktop installer for the actual CPU/OS, or the documented Homebrew route on Mac. On first run configure a supported model key or the separate credit service under Settings → Keys. Source development uses the contributing guide. [Source 1](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Choose the official desktop installer for the actual CPU/OS, or the documented Homebrew route on Mac. On first run configure a supported model key or the separate credit service under Settings → Keys. Source development uses the contributing guide.

```sh
brew install hormuz-labs/tap/manul
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Create a project from footage you may edit and configure its model provider.
2. Give a bounded edit note; inspect the proposed before/after version and any still frames or transcript involved.
3. Keep or send back the change, adjust text manually and export a short review before a larger production.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Packaged Electron desktop app and bundled media helpers. Optional model endpoints/credits and phone pairing are separate services; Android remains a source build until its public listing is available.
- **Platforms:** Official Apple Silicon/Intel macOS, Windows 10 1903+/11 x64, Debian/Ubuntu and Linux AppImage routes are documented. Phone support has distinct Android/iPhone limitations; it is not equivalent to a standalone mobile renderer.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/NOTICE)

- **Code:** GPL-3.0
- **Weights:** GPL-3.0-or-later is described by the project; the full GPL grant was reviewed. Native helpers, models, fonts, FFmpeg and any media assets have their own notices. Model keys or AgentShot credits do not come with the software grant.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows GPL-3.0. GPL-3.0-or-later is described by the project; the full GPL grant was reviewed. Native helpers, models, fonts, FFmpeg and any media assets have their own notices. Model keys or AgentShot credits do not come with the software grant.
- **Cost:** Local rendering resources plus separately chosen Claude, Gemini, OpenAI-compatible or credit-service usage.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/hormuz-labs/agentshot/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Limitations

### Install

The documented Mac package uses an isolated uv-tool environment and includes the built web UI. Install FFmpeg/uv, use the README’s pinned v0.5.1 recipe and start caption-studio. Docker has a separate linux/amd64 CPU route; its raw command maps host 8768 to container 8788, despite a following README line mentioning 8788. [Source 1](https://github.com/suyaleo/Caption_Studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/suyaleo/Caption_Studio/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

2. The documented Mac package uses an isolated uv-tool environment and includes the built web UI. Install FFmpeg/uv, use the README’s pinned v0.5.1 recipe and start caption-studio. Docker has a separate linux/amd64 CPU route; its raw command maps host 8768 to container 8788, despite a following README line mentioning 8788.

```sh
brew install uv ffmpeg-full
```


```sh
uv tool install --python 3.11 "caption-studio[asr-macos] @ git+https://github.com/suyaleo/Caption_Studio.git@v0.5.1"
```


```sh
caption-studio
```

2. Choose the supported local ASR route, inspect flagged passages and correct words and timings.
3. Translate only if separately configured, preview the chosen subtitle styling and export SRT/VTT or hard-subbed MP4.

python scripts/setup/download_weights.py

python scripts/image_to_hair.py character.png --out results/my_character --n 4
```


```sh
python -m hairdeck.vrm.export results/my_character/character 0
```

### How it uses AI

A vision-capable agent drives local generation, GroundingDINO/SAM layer separation and iterative cleanup into a YAML-specified 2D character rig with procedural spring/keyframe playback. [Source](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/docs/pipeline.md)

### Introduction

A vision-capable agent drives local generation, GroundingDINO/SAM layer separation and iterative cleanup into a YAML-specified 2D character rig with procedural spring/keyframe playback. [Source 1](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/docs/pipeline.md)

### What it is good for

Create reviewable layered game or interactive-story characters while retaining masks, point tables, source images and an editable rig specification. [Source 1](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/docs/pipeline.md)

### Demo & examples

The supplied Mira example has 13 layers and offline previews. Playing that bundled example does not demonstrate that the complete neural generation/cleanup pipeline works on a new installation. [Source 1](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/docs/pipeline.md)

### Install

Use Python >=3.10 with a configured local AUTOMATIC1111-compatible API and its documented segmentation extension; supply separately permitted weights. Install the package/PSD extra from the published package or source checkout and start from the supplied character spec. [Source 1](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/docs/pipeline.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Python >=3.10 with a configured local AUTOMATIC1111-compatible API and its documented segmentation extension; supply separately permitted weights. Install the package/PSD extra from the published package or source checkout and start from the supplied character spec.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig.git
```


```sh
cd img2rig
```


```sh
python -m pip install -e ".[psd]"
```


```sh
cp examples/character.yaml mychar.yaml
```


```sh
img2rig gen --spec mychar.yaml
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/docs/pipeline.md)

1. Configure local model endpoints and a small permitted character brief in the YAML spec.
2. Generate candidates, inspect contact sheets and refine the segmentation point tables and cleanup through the documented visual loop.
3. Export the layered rig, test its motions in the supplied player and integrate it into the target runtime after visual review.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/docs/pipeline.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python >=3.10, optional PSD support, a separate model-serving WebUI/segmentation extension and a vision-capable external agent. C++ runtime is header-only; browser playback is a distinct lightweight route.
- **Platforms:** Python/local-HTTP authoring and C++/browser playback are documented. A universally tested native OS/GPU matrix and numeric model RAM/VRAM/storage minima are not documented.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 4](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/docs/pipeline.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Complete Apache-2.0 code grant and NOTICE reviewed. The model-serving WebUI, segmentation extension and user-provided checkpoints have separate terms; the bundled Mira rig is identified as CC0. No Live2D SDK or Live2D-format export is included.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Complete Apache-2.0 code grant and NOTICE reviewed. The model-serving WebUI, segmentation extension and user-provided checkpoints have separate terms; the bundled Mira rig is identified as CC0. No Live2D SDK or Live2D-format export is included.
- **Cost:** Local model computation and any selected external agent/model-service access.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/docs/pipeline.md)

### Limitations

Rigid layers and springs approximate deformation; large poses use keyframe swaps. Per-character segmentation coordinates require a fresh visual loop. Runtime playback and alpha/file checks cannot prove correct anatomy, clean masks or production animation quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/docs/pipeline.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/SSnemo/img2rig/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. VRM export currently accepts VRM 0.x rather than VRM 1.0. A plain GLB does not carry standardized spring physics. The documented workflow 1.0.1 widget-order migration needs the updated graph or replacement nodes. Card geometry and a successful export do not establish strand-level realism or production-ready simulation.

### How it uses AI

A separate ComfyUI integration runs HairDeck’s image-conditioned diffusion pipeline and exposes hair-card generation, color, spring-bone chains, GLB and VRM export as graph nodes. ### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/visualbruno/ComfyUI-HairDeck/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/visualbruno/ComfyUI-HairDeck/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/visualbruno/ComfyUI-HairDeck/blob/main/nodes.py)

- **Hardware:** The documented default is offload enabled. Model components total about 7.7 GB before Blender/environment/media; this is not total disk space. The author reports 6.7 GB peak VRAM in one offload test and about 10 GB without offload; neither is a universal minimum. System RAM is unspecified.
- **Software:** An existing compatible ComfyUI/Python/PyTorch CUDA environment, git and the listed requirements. Matching bpy or an external Blender is required for rigged/VRM exports. Required model downloads and Blender are separate from the node package.
- **Platforms:** The integration explicitly requires an NVIDIA GPU. Its developer test used an RTX 3080 Laptop 16 GB, Python 3.13, PyTorch 2.10 and CUDA 13. Standalone HairDeck’s MPS/ROCm support does not establish this plugin’s Mac/AMD compatibility.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Scope: Prototype a conversational exhibition host or speaking character with your own permitted portrait and voice, while separating simple idle animation from actual lip synthesis.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-13. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/lanzhu1993/Digital-Human/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The developer publishes interface/architecture and character-production guides. - **Reproducible setup · documented:** Clone the official source, create a Python 3.11+ environment, install requirements and copy the example configuration into a private environment file. The default model route is mock; configure a real compatible model to test AI answers. Edge speech still accesses Microsoft even in mock mode. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/lanzhu1993/Digital-Human/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/lanzhu1993/Digital-Human/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Complete MIT application grant reviewed. Speech engines, LiveTalking, model weights, browser/system voices and user-provided face/voice media retain separate grants; none of the private company avatar assets are included. Local server resources and separately selected language/speech-model access; offline model hosting has its own hardware/storage requirements. [Source](https://github.com/lanzhu1993/Digital-Human/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/lanzhu1993/Digital-Human/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. An idle video with a volume-reactive halo is not lip-sync generation. Mock replies do not demonstrate model inference. Knowledge retrieval can fall back to ordinary chat, and noisy playback can mis-trigger voice interruption. Complete RAM/VRAM/storage limits are not documented.

### How it uses AI

A FastAPI/browser character interface streams a model answer sentence by sentence, requests speech for each sentence and queues playback; optional LiveTalking supplies a separate lip-sync engine. [Source](https://github.com/lanzhu1993/Digital-Human/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/lanzhu1993/Digital-Human/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

Generated images may drift despite reference prompting; tracing is computed geometry rather than a model learning a real artist’s strokes. Fine screens, hatching, gradients or broken outlines can disrupt region coloring. Provider free quotas change and are not a guaranteed zero-cost production plan.

### How it uses AI

A model-assisted script-to-illustration workflow obtains static images and neural narration, then computes line tracing, region coloring and FFmpeg composition into a hand-drawing-style video. [Source](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

```


```sh
python scripts/xm_handdraw.py styles
```


```sh
python scripts/xm_handdraw.py providers
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

1. Prepare a short script and a storyboard with concrete per-scene subjects; review the wording before requesting speech or images.
2. Generate/review static illustrations with cleared character/style references, then use the documented staged build so individual scenes can be corrected.
3. Inspect the draw-on/paint order and final captions. For full-frame color, review the documented paint-bg and zero-margin behavior; check the completed duration/audio rather than trusting a file’s existence.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

- **Hardware:** The developer explicitly states no local generation GPU is required for this hosted-image/CPU-render route. Minimum RAM and total disk space are not published.
- **Software:** Python 3.9+, Pillow, NumPy, edge-tts and FFmpeg/libass, a subtitle font and a supported provider key/configuration. The public project does not run a diffusion model locally.
- **Platforms:** Output testing is explicitly documented only on Windows 11. Cross-platform code paths exist, but macOS/Linux were not tested by the author; their likely compatibility is not treated as confirmed support.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Complete MIT code grant reviewed; bundled font uses SIL OFL 1.1 and example illustrations are separately declared MIT by the author. Image-provider, speech, reference-character and downstream asset terms remain independent.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Complete MIT code grant reviewed; bundled font uses SIL OFL 1.1 and example illustrations are separately declared MIT by the author. Image-provider, speech, reference-character and downstream asset terms remain independent.
- **Cost:** Local CPU/storage plus whichever image/speech service is configured. Developer descriptions of free quotas were not independently established as current entitlements.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

### Limitations

Generated images may drift despite reference prompting; tracing is computed geometry rather than a model learning a real artist’s strokes. Fine screens, hatching, gradients or broken outlines can disrupt region coloring. Provider free quotas change and are not a guaranteed zero-cost production plan. [Source 1](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/sratour/xm-handdraw-video/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

### How it uses AI

A documented creative-agent workflow has a model author editable OpenSCAD code, render it through an MCP bridge and iterate before a separate slicer/physical-print step. [Source](https://github.com/TS00/describe-to-print/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A documented creative-agent workflow has a model author editable OpenSCAD code, render it through an MCP bridge and iterate before a separate slicer/physical-print step. [Source 1](https://github.com/TS00/describe-to-print/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

The supplied Linux/Flatpak recipe installs OpenSCAD and Bambu Studio, exposes an OpenSCAD CLI wrapper, then installs the separate openscad-mcp server in its own Python environment. Point the selected AI editor at that bridge and set its workspace to the intended design folder. [Source 1](https://github.com/TS00/describe-to-print/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. The supplied Linux/Flatpak recipe installs OpenSCAD and Bambu Studio, exposes an OpenSCAD CLI wrapper, then installs the separate openscad-mcp server in its own Python environment. Point the selected AI editor at that bridge and set its workspace to the intended design folder.

```sh
flatpak install --user -y flathub org.openscad.OpenSCAD com.bambulab.BambuStudio
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/TS00/describe-to-print/blob/main/README.md)

1. Describe one small part with units, dimensions and intended fit, then inspect the agent-authored SCAD source.
2. Render through the separately configured bridge, revise dimensions and compare multiple views.
3. Export a checked STL and inspect it in the actual slicer/printer profile before a separately authorized physical print; keep source code and test measurements.

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/TS00/describe-to-print/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** An actual compatible FDM printer is needed only for the physical result. The README supplies no minimum workstation RAM, VRAM or storage.
- **Software:** Documented stack: an AI editor such as Cursor, OpenSCAD, a separate Python openscad-mcp installation and a slicer. Flatpak is the published setup route; the printer connection and its permissions are separate.
- **Platforms:** Linux/Flatpak setup is documented. The example printer is a Bambu Lab A1; the author says other FDM printers may be used, but a tested cross-printer or Mac/Windows setup matrix is not established here.

- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Complete MIT project grant reviewed. The AI editor, MCP bridge, OpenSCAD, Bambu Studio and any printer/firmware/material have their own licenses and requirements; an STL is not a material or safety approval.
- **Cost:** Chosen agent service, workstation resources, printer and material costs are separate.

## Performance Remix — Wan Fast Route

Video, animation & film · Motion capture & character animation · Avatars, digital humans & lip sync

The repository was created on 2026-10-30. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Explore a permitted person/pet performance substitution using the documented Wan2.2 Fun Control route, with human approval of the still before the slower video stage.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-13. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/performance-remix/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author publishes a two-minute walkthrough, character/object before-afters and measured timing/memory examples. These are developer results; Scout did not reproduce them or assess identity fidelity. [Source](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/performance-remix/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/performance-remix/blob/main/docs/models.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install the documented Windows NVIDIA ComfyUI setup, the Performance Remix node pack and only the optional packs/models required for the chosen route. The web app additionally needs its comfy-api-proxy service and Node dependencies, each started in a separate terminal. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/performance-remix/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/performance-remix/blob/main/docs/models.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Complete MIT app/workflow grant reviewed. The author lists Wan2.2 Fun Control as Apache-2.0, separately from Qwen image preparation, face models and optional upscaling. Viggle/MiniMax H3 has territorial/commercial restrictions and LTX object models have community terms; those are not granted by MIT and are not the selected Wan route. Local GPU/RAM/storage, separately permitted models and cleared footage; this selected route needs no hosted generation API. [Source](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/performance-remix/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/performance-remix/blob/main/docs/models.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Developer tests used a 32 GB GPU with an imposed 16 GB memory limit; actual 16/24 GB cards were not tested. One subject and up to 30 seconds are documented, subject to RAM limits. Other object workflows can lose an object after hand-off; the author reports only two successful holds out of seven seeds in one example. No automatic quality recommendation follows.

### How it uses AI

A local ComfyUI/web workflow paints an approved replacement look into a clip’s first frame, then uses a selected motion model to animate that look against the driving video. Look models are about 31 GB; Wan route about 31 GB plus optional 16.7 GB face-pass and shared files. These are component/author limits, not total workspace minima.
- **Software:** ComfyUI 0.34+, Git, Node.js 22+ and Python 3.10+ for comfy-api-proxy. ComfyUI, proxy (8189) and app (3000) are separate services. Models are downloaded independently into the documented folders.
- **Platforms:** Windows NVIDIA route is documented/tested by the author. Linux instructions are explicitly untested; macOS is unsupported for these NVIDIA workflows.

The README supplies node contracts and developer clip timing/identity observations. They document intended behavior and specific experiments, not independent quality validation. [Source 1](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/comfyui-performance-remix/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Clone into ComfyUI/custom_nodes and install requirements with the Python environment actually running ComfyUI. The Windows portable recipe explicitly uses python_embeded. Restart ComfyUI and load a route whose models and weights are separately available and permitted. [Source 1](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/comfyui-performance-remix/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone into ComfyUI/custom_nodes and install requirements with the Python environment actually running ComfyUI. The Windows portable recipe explicitly uses python_embeded. Restart ComfyUI and load a route whose models and weights are separately available and permitted.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/UmutGuzel/comfyui-performance-remix.git ComfyUI/custom_nodes/comfyui-performance-remix
```


```sh
python -m pip install -r ComfyUI/custom_nodes/comfyui-performance-remix/requirements.txt
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/comfyui-performance-remix/blob/main/README.md)

1. Choose a documented Wan/SCAIL motion or enhancement route and wire a short permitted reference/driving clip.
2. Check frame size, source/output frame rates, masks and repeated-frame overlap before sampling.
3. Inspect joins and face/object composites; save a short file with the original audio and verify synchronization before processing a longer clip.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/comfyui-performance-remix/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The README reports examples on a 32 GB GPU, including an 18.6 GB peak for one Wan Animate case. A universal minimum VRAM/RAM/disk is not documented; requirements depend on model, resolution, segment length and output duration.
- **Software:** ComfyUI 0.34+ and opencv-python 4.8+ are documented; model-specific nodes/weights and encoders remain separate. Bundled YuNet is covered by its own MIT notice.
- **Platforms:** Windows portable and Linux/manual-install recipes are provided. Mac/AMD support is not established by these recipes or the separate parent app.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/comfyui-performance-remix/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/comfyui-performance-remix/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Complete MIT node-package grant reviewed. The README separately identifies MIT SCAIL-2 and Apache-2.0 Wan model families; optional SeedVR2, LTX, segmentation and other model/runtime terms remain independent.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Complete MIT node-package grant reviewed. The README separately identifies MIT SCAIL-2 and Apache-2.0 Wan model families; optional SeedVR2, LTX, segmentation and other model/runtime terms remain independent.
- **Cost:** Local compute/storage and separately licensed model assets; optional cloud deployments are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/comfyui-performance-remix/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Only the current processing segment may fit in GPU memory while the accumulated output still needs storage/host memory. No-face detection returns center crops or pass-through behavior, not a verified successful identity edit. Long-clip, join and similarity claims are developer observations rather than universal guarantees. [Source 1](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/comfyui-performance-remix/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/comfyui-performance-remix)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/UmutGuzel/comfyui-performance-remix/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

A ReShade add-on keeps a temporal styled framebuffer and optionally learned material-UV history, sending coherent frames to neural style-transfer or prompt-driven inference backends before final composition. [Source](https://github.com/MiLO83/NeuralPass/blob/main/README.md)

Full-training status and independent visual/metric equivalence remain unverified.

### How it uses AI

A per-material neural trainer optimizes block-compressed feature textures and a small MLP, exports BC6H DDS/FP16 decoder artifacts and independently reloads them for reference reconstruction. ### Install

Prepare a compatible Linux PyTorch CUDA environment, install the requirements and compile the separate BC6H reference decoder with CMake/GCC. Build a correctly encoded nine-channel material target plus its identity/hash file before the documented short debug training run. [Source 1](https://github.com/zhr123321a/bcf6/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zhr123321a/bcf6/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/zhr123321a/bcf6/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Prepare a compatible Linux PyTorch CUDA environment, install the requirements and compile the separate BC6H reference decoder with CMake/GCC. Build a correctly encoded nine-channel material target plus its identity/hash file before the documented short debug training run.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/zhr123321a/bcf6.git
```


```sh
cd bcf6
```


```sh
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
cmake -S . -B build -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release
```


```sh
cmake --build build -j 2
```


```sh
python -m bcf6.train --help
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/zhr123321a/bcf6/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zhr123321a/bcf6/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/zhr123321a/bcf6/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Prepare permitted diffuse, normal and ORM targets with the documented channel semantics, dimensions and identity SHA; do not silently linearize/rescale/fill missing channels.
2. Use the short 45-update workflow only to check pipeline/save/reload, retaining strict-pass warnings. It is not a convergence or quality benchmark.
3. Inspect saved DDS/decoder/metadata and replay metrics. For a longer run preserve the exact source, target and full training configuration; do not confuse the PyTorch reference reconstruction with a hardware-sampled realtime renderer.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/zhr123321a/bcf6/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zhr123321a/bcf6/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/zhr123321a/bcf6/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Requirements list NumPy >=1.24, Pillow >=9 and Torch >=2.2 as lower bounds, not a tested version matrix. The stated Linux validation used PyTorch 2.7.1+cu118 and GCC 11.4; CMake builds the reference decoder.
- **Platforms:** Linux-first implementation and acceptance. Windows/MSVC instructions exist, but Windows computation and cross-platform artifact acceptance remain pending. CPU is a debug path, not the suggested full-training route.

- **Creative results · documented:** The author shows a five-second reading-corner camera move and desktop screenshots. These illustrate a workflow; this review did not reproduce the animation. [Source](https://github.com/EsscapadaBuilds/SceneDirector/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Clone the official repository, install Python 3.10+, then use the documented setup command. It can download Blender and install a Claude skill; review its options first. Configure a tool-capable local Ollama model or a hosted provider in a private .env. The Windows desktop executable still needs the repository and Python beside it. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/EsscapadaBuilds/SceneDirector/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.

- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT covers SceneDirector code. Blender, Python packages, chosen LLM weights, asset licenses and hosted services retain their own terms. Poly Haven and ambientCG assets are documented as CC0. Claude access is separate. Local model/render compute or external LLM fees; optional Tripo/Meshy mesh generation uses credits. A provider free tier is not a permanent cost guarantee. [Source](https://github.com/EsscapadaBuilds/SceneDirector/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Reference images are art-direction targets, not automatic full-scene 3D reconstruction. Complex hair, cloth and IK still need human work; Mixamo is manual. Paid Tripo/Meshy connectors were tested against mocks, which does not establish live provider success. Free tiers can be limited and Pollinations references may carry watermarks.

### How it uses AI

A tool-calling language model directs headless Blender: it proposes references, asks for approval, assembles assets, inspects screenshots and produces editable scenes and renders. [Source](https://github.com/EsscapadaBuilds/SceneDirector/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A tool-calling language model directs headless Blender: it proposes references, asks for approval, assembles assets, inspects screenshots and produces editable scenes and renders. [Source 1](https://github.com/EsscapadaBuilds/SceneDirector/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the documented Mac or Windows release, or build the Rust source. Open the studio and connect an already authenticated agent through the localhost MCP endpoint; a bridge is supplied for stdio clients. GUI/Bluetooth source builds use their feature flags and platform libraries.

```sh
cargo build --release --features gui,ble
```


```sh
claude mcp add --transport http zigzag http://127.0.0.1:7878/mcp
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/NOTICE)

1. Set fabric, hoop and physical dimensions before asking the agent to compose a small design.
2. Inspect stitches, density and Issues, then adjust spacing, underlay and sequence.
3. Export the machine format and perform a small fabric test before a final garment.

### How it uses AI

An unofficial port runs a four-step MOSS image transformer on an AMD XDNA 2 NPU, with CPU text encoding and mixed CPU/NPU image decoding. [Source](https://github.com/tomhda/moss-image-ryzen-ai-npu/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An unofficial port runs a four-step MOSS image transformer on an AMD XDNA 2 NPU, with CPU text encoding and mixed CPU/NPU image decoding. [Source 1](https://github.com/tomhda/moss-image-ryzen-ai-npu/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Exploring explicitly documented 512-square and 1024-square text-to-image inference on a particular Ryzen AI machine without claiming all-NPU execution. [Source 1](https://github.com/tomhda/moss-image-ryzen-ai-npu/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The author supplies image samples, per-stage logs and repeated timing measurements on one Ryzen AI 7 PRO 350. Reported text rendering includes misspellings, and identical prompts/seeds differ from the official bf16 pipeline. [Source 1](https://github.com/tomhda/moss-image-ryzen-ai-npu/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install AMD Ryzen AI Software 1.8.0 and its driver, conda/Miniforge, PowerShell 7 and Git. The size-specific setup creates two environments and downloads pinned/hash-checked model parts; run the matching doctor command before the first example. [Source 1](https://github.com/tomhda/moss-image-ryzen-ai-npu/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install AMD Ryzen AI Software 1.8.0 and its driver, conda/Miniforge, PowerShell 7 and Git. The size-specific setup creates two environments and downloads pinned/hash-checked model parts; run the matching doctor command before the first example.

```sh
pwsh -NoProfile -File .\setup.ps1 -Size 512
```


```sh
pwsh -NoProfile -File .\doctor.ps1 -Size 512
```


```sh
pwsh -NoProfile -File .\gen.ps1 -Items .\examples\one.json -Out .\outputs\first -Size 512
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/tomhda/moss-image-ryzen-ai-npu/blob/main/README.md)

1. Choose the supported size and inspect the pinned model manifest and local paths.
2. Generate one documented JSON prompt and inspect PNG, latents and per-stage logs.
3. Batch several prompts to amortize loading, respecting the documented token limits and exit codes.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/tomhda/moss-image-ryzen-ai-npu/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Author-tested AMD Ryzen AI 7 PRO 350 with 32 GB RAM. Documented free-disk requirement about 50 GB for 512 only and 54 GB for both sizes; 16 GB RAM was not tested. No separate GPU/VRAM minimum is established.
- **Software:** Verified author configuration: Windows 11 Home build 26200, Ryzen AI Software 1.8.0, NPU driver 32.0.203.329. Text environment uses Python 3.12.16 and a separately pinned CPU PyTorch stack.
- **Platforms:** Only the stated Windows/XDNA 2 machine/driver configuration was tested by the author. Other XDNA 2 CPUs, 16 GB machines, other drivers, Mac and Linux are not established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/tomhda/moss-image-ryzen-ai-npu/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/tomhda/moss-image-ryzen-ai-npu/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 covers scripts. The README identifies the MOSS model, four-step LoRA and AMD FLUX decoder as Apache-2.0; these are separate artifacts and their full individual grants were not all independently rechecked here. AMD runtime licensing is separate.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 covers scripts. The README identifies the MOSS model, four-step LoRA and AMD FLUX decoder as Apache-2.0; these are separate artifacts and their full individual grants were not all independently rechecked here. AMD runtime licensing is separate.
- **Cost:** Local compute/storage and separately licensed AMD software; no inference service account is required by the documented local route.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/tomhda/moss-image-ryzen-ai-npu/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Many operations remain on CPU. 1024 decoding uses AMD FLUX.2 rather than the MOSS CPU decoder by default. Deterministic repeatability within this port is not equivalence to the official pipeline or proof of image quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/tomhda/moss-image-ryzen-ai-npu/blob/main/README.md)

User-entered provenance is evidence-backed curation, not automatic legal clearance. Local compute plus optional external model/service use; owning/indexing a pack does not establish permission for every project. [Source](https://github.com/digitalhobbit/assetcat/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

### Hardware & software

- **Hardware:** Embedding model download about 1.5 GB; an example 200,000-image vector index about 350 MB plus thumbnails/database. Suggested vision-model downloads are about 5 or 16 GB. Universal RAM/VRAM and total working-disk minima are not documented.
- **Software:** Python 3.12+; PyTorch for the AI extra. SigLIP 2 downloads on first use, with CPU, Apple Silicon or NVIDIA execution. Pack tagging uses LM Studio, Ollama, llama.cpp or a separately authorized cloud endpoint.
- **Platforms:** macOS, Windows and Linux are documented. Windows NVIDIA use needs a CUDA-compatible PyTorch build rather than assuming default CPU packages provide GPU execution.

- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. The full project code grant was reviewed. Gemma/SigLIP model terms, LM Studio or other host access and every indexed asset license remain separate. User-entered provenance is evidence-backed curation, not automatic legal clearance.

### Introduction

A local creative workspace connects separately configured model APIs to scripts, storyboards, images, video, speech, comic layout and FFmpeg timelines. [Source 1](https://github.com/3384824804/Spring-Workbench/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Organize a short episode or motion comic with editable storyboards, generation task history, adopted asset versions and locally exported media. [Source 1](https://github.com/3384824804/Spring-Workbench/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README documents a Windows source workflow and module capability table. These are scope/setup descriptions; this review did not generate an episode or verify provider compatibility. [Source 1](https://github.com/3384824804/Spring-Workbench/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Obtain the Apache-licensed source and use Node24 on the documented Windows x64 route. Install the locked npm dependencies, build and start the server, then configure your provider/Base URL/key/model privately. FFmpeg/ffprobe and relevant model capabilities are prerequisites for the selected media/export stages. [Source 1](https://github.com/3384824804/Spring-Workbench/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Obtain the Apache-licensed source and use Node24 on the documented Windows x64 route. Install the locked npm dependencies, build and start the server, then configure your provider/Base URL/key/model privately. FFmpeg/ffprobe and relevant model capabilities are prerequisites for the selected media/export stages.

```sh
npm ci
```


```sh
npm run build
```


```sh
npm run server
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/3384824804/Spring-Workbench/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open 127.0.0.1:5178 and create a project.
2. Configure only the model routes actually supported by your provider; draft/review a script and storyboard.
3. Generate a small set of assets, inspect the accepted versions and timeline, then test a short H.264 export.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/3384824804/Spring-Workbench/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Author-tested Windows x64, Node24 and Chrome/Edge. FFmpeg/ffprobe are needed for editing/export; TTS/ASR use remote APIs or separately installed local runners.
- **Platforms:** Windows source route is author-tested. Other source platforms are not accepted/tested in the documented release; Windows executable packaging is Windows-only.

## Excluded and unresolved findings

Research notes only; these do not enter the eligible tool library.

- **HairGS** · excluded: The root MIT file does not supersede the additional non-commercial Gaussian-Splatting terms that the README says apply to the combined distribution. It is creative research context, not an admitted open-source application. [Source](https://github.com/yimin-pan/hair-gs)
- **DiffLocks** · excluded: The complete grant limits use to non-commercial scientific/educational/artistic purposes and restricts redistribution. A separately MIT-licensed subcomponent does not make the complete project open-source. [Source](https://github.com/Meshcapade/difflocks)
- **Crayotter** · excluded: The full root grant is PolyForm Noncommercial; paper wording about openness does not replace that software restriction. [Source](https://github.com/idwts/Crayotter)
- **IPHG / CT2Hair / HairPort** · excluded: The inspected root grants are CC BY-NC or CC BY-NC-ND. These non-commercial/no-derivatives restrictions do not qualify as open-source software grants. [Source](https://github.com/facebookresearch/iphg) [Source](https://github.com/facebookresearch/CT2Hair) [Source](https://github.com/deepmancer/HairPort)
- **SAFE-Hair / NeuralHDHair / HairGPT** · needs-license-review: A promised release or paper/project page does not establish a complete released and licensed application. Actual inference availability and full grants remain unresolved for these candidates. [Source](https://github.com/SAFE-Hair/Code) [Source](https://github.com/KeyuWu-CS/NeuralHDHair) [Source](https://haiminluo.github.io/hairgpt/)
- **HyFluid / PICT smoke** · needs-license-review: Actual research code was located, but complete software grants were not returned for the inspected repositories. Smoke reconstruction examples were not substituted for license evidence. [Source](https://github.com/y-zheng18/HyFluid) [Source](https://github.com/19reborn/PICT_smoke)
- **VRMHairMaker** · needs-license-review: The project has HairDeck-related implementation but no complete own software grant was returned. An upstream Apache grant is not automatically a grant for the wrapper. [Source](https://github.com/camenduru/VRMHairMaker)
- **StrandHead** · needs-license-review: An MIT root sits alongside borrowed NeuralHaircut material with separate non-commercial terms. The full combined source/provenance scope requires resolution before admission. [Source](https://github.com/XiaokunSun/StrandHead)
- **FengLairh ComfyUI-HairDeck distribution** · needs-license-review: The adapter root is Apache-2.0, but this distribution vendors SAM 3 and DINOv3 software under custom licenses covering code as well as weights, including use restrictions. It is held pending a clearly separated open-code scope; mirrors and checksum validation do not remove license conditions. [Source](https://github.com/FengLairh/ComfyUI-HairDeck/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source](https://github.com/FengLairh/ComfyUI-HairDeck/blob/main/_vendor/hairdeck_sam3/LICENSE) [Source](https://github.com/FengLairh/ComfyUI-HairDeck/blob/main/_vendor/hairdeck_dinov3/LICENSE.md)
- **Comfy-WebUI / wan-pilot** · needs-license-review: The full Apache-looking grants alter the standard liability wording. They were not automatically treated as recognized Apache-2.0 merely because metadata identifies that SPDX value. [Source](https://github.com/RioShiina47/comfy-webui) [Source](https://github.com/EngineerAbdullahBinZafar/wan-pilot)
- **Mia full-guide completion** · needs-license-review: The application root is Apache, but bundled model/graph components have separate community and open-source grants and some models are non-commercial or research-limited. The existing historical screened entry is preserved; this edition does not assert an all-Apache bundle or publish a completed guide without resolving the selected component scope. [Source](https://github.com/Quentincls/mia)
- **AgenticCAD** · excluded: The primary page explicitly states free for non-commercial use. Creative relevance and CAD export do not establish an open-source software grant. [Source](https://agenticcad.github.io/agenticcad/)
- **Xotion Studio** · excluded: The complete GitLab grant is PolyForm Noncommercial, so this otherwise relevant video project is excluded from open-source software results. [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/xotion-studio/-/raw/main/LICENSE)
- **AEGIR / Fol.AI / PHOSA / FORM** · needs-license-review: The primary pages describe relevant research but announce code/data as coming soon or leave release availability unresolved. Paper/video examples are not evidence that a licensed implementation has shipped. [Source](https://darkgeekms.github.io/projects/aegir/) [Source](https://ispamm.github.io/FolAI/) [Source](https://naaapi.github.io/PHOSA/) [Source](https://form-robots.github.io/)
- **RiTa v4** · excluded: The primary site explicitly describes the toolkit as AI-free. It is useful generative-language context, but conventional language generation alone does not meet this library AI-only scope. [Source](https://rednoise.org/rita/)
- **Missing or unreleased implementations** · needs-license-review: Some searched repositories contained only architecture/docs, planned neural features or screenshots/binary releases. Those were not admitted merely from an AI label, root license or executable download; raw candidate details remain local for later source checks. [Source](https://github.com/thatsbowen/minimax-h3-director)
- **MoneyPrinterPlus** · excluded: The complete root LICENSE appends personal/educational-only and no-commercial-use conditions to a GPL-looking text. These restrictions exclude the combined grant despite GitHub GPL metadata. [Source](https://github.com/ddean2009/MoneyPrinterPlus/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **OpenDesign** · needs-license-review: The full license changes Apache trademark wording and the additional-liability/support section. Its Apache metadata and large star count were not substituted for a recognized unmodified grant review. [Source](https://github.com/nexu-io/open-design/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **TeaCache composite grant** · needs-license-review: The full root license includes a large composite set of upstream notices/grants. A complete composite review remains pending; the first Apache header was not treated as the whole license. [Source](https://github.com/ali-vilab/TeaCache/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **No-Slop Color** · excluded: The official current repository metadata marks this project archived. It is not admitted as a currently maintained discovery; the reviewed MIT license and creative-use source evidence remain retained locally. Archive status alone does not revoke its license or judge all historical output. [Source](https://github.com/victornasar/no-slop-color)
